2.11. Difficulties encounter in implementation of the Act: Some of the States have
furnished the details of problems facing in implementing the provision of RBD Act and State
Rules in the State/UT. The brief points given by the State Governments in their annual report
is summarized below:-

 Andhra Pradesh: No fresh sanction of posts of Statistical staff was made in 2021. Sub
Registrars posts are also vacant in many municipalities. The lack of budget under CRS and MCCD
towards trainings, Publicity, inspection and printing of forms affects the successful implementation
of RBD act in the state.

 Arunachal Pradesh: Inadequate infrastructure is the major problem for implementation
of the Act. Further, due to shortage of vital statistics staff, lack of proper communication,
illiteracy among the villagers, villages being scattered in far flung areas, etc. caused the issue
of under reporting.

 Assam: Lack of awareness to private nursing homes regarding their liabilities on each
event of birth and death. Non availability of CRS fund in state budget for implementation of
RBD Act, 1969. There is shortage of computer and other accessories and skilled personnel in
registration units.

 Goa: Damage caused to old registration record of 1970 to 1980 due to frequent handling.

 Gujarat: Most of the posts of Taluka/District Statistical Assistants are vacant out of total
sanctioned posts. Registrars are not utilizing the powers vested under the section 15 of the
RBD Act, 1969 regarding corrections and cancellation of entries in birth and death record.
Lack of awareness about the need and importance of registration of births and deaths and
lack of registration procedure knowledge among the public.

 Himachal Pradesh: Local Registrars cum Panchayats Secretaries in concerned
Panchayats are not posting vital events online regularly. There is shortage of manpower in
some of the registration units across the State. There have been no financial assistances from
Center & State Govt. to meet out the expenditure towards Information, Education &
Communication (IEC) and other CRS related activities during the year 2021.

                                                                          27
 Jharkhand: Some difficulties are encounter in implementing the provisions of RBD Act
and Rules in respect of delayed registration provisions. Difficulties in timely submission of
reports and supervision due to lack of Block Statistical Supervisor at block level. Difficulties
in reporting events after 21 days of occurrence in ORGI uniform CRS software.

 Karnataka: Inadequacy of staff at all levels. Lack of administrative authority to the Chief
Registrar over registration functionaries.

 Kerala: Pre occupation of Registrars and other functionaries with other duties. Damage
caused to very old registration record. Non registration of large chunk of infant and child
deaths particularly in rural areas. Inclusion of details without care in registration by the
public and request for correction of particulars in the registers as entered in other
documents pertaining to the individuals.

 Madhya Pradesh: Vacant posts of Assistant Statistical Officer, Statistical Clerks and
Clerks in various Municipal Corporations and Municipalities. Lack of control over Notifies in
the rural areas.

 Maharashtra: In rural area due to illiteracy, lack of transportation in hilly areas, and
public unawareness are the hurdles in achieving the goal of 100% registration. The Gram
Sevaks who is Ex-officio Registrar of births and deaths, has to cover many villages due to
vacant posts.

 Manipur: No difficulties encountered in implementing RBD Act 1969.

 Mizoram: The geographic location of the State has affected the registration process. Gap
in communication and transportation facilities hampers timely reports making it difficult to
consistently maintain 100% level of reporting.
 Nagaland: Inadequate postal, transportation and communication facilities. Lack of
remuneration for CRS functionaries and low level of awareness are major difficulties in the
State.

 Odisha: There is shortage of staff/manpower in the State.

 Punjab: There is a shortage of Staff at every level in the State and associated
departments are facing difficulty in managing various affairs of registration of births and
deaths.

 Tamil Nadu: Non-availability of funds for sustaining the registration of births and deaths
through online facility in terms of software and hardware, inspection of registration units,
organizing training programme for CRS functionaries, Publicity on CRS at
State/Districts/Registration Unit levels etc.

                                                                          28
 Telangana: COVID 19 is one of the major cause in under reporting. Lack of Sub registrars
and statistical staff in Municipalities. Large number of vacant posts of Panchayats
Secretaries.

 Tripura: Difficulties encountered in implementing the RBD Act and Rules made
thereunder particularly in case of delayed registration. Issues have been arising related to
online registration of births and deaths. There is shortage of Staff at every level in the State,
shortage of machinery and network also.

 Uttarakhand: Due to lack of manpower, publicity, remote rural area and training,
difficulties encounter in implementation of RBD Act, 1969. Vacant post in birth-death
registration cadre of Health department.

 Uttar Pradesh: Difficulties in reporting events was faced by general public due to Covid-
19 pandemic. Vacant post of Assistant Statistical officer, Additional Statistical officer etc.

 West Bengal: As such there is no difficulty in implementation of the act at field level.
However, at time problem was faced in some places due to shortage of manpower.

 Puducherry: No difficulties encountered in implementing RBD Act 1969 since
compulsory Registration of vital events was prevalent during the French Rule.

2.12. Circulars/Guidelines issued by the Office of Registrar General, India: The
following circulars/ guidelines during the year 2021 were issued from ORGI: -

 "Procedure for Registration of Death of Missing persons in the affected areas of
Uttarakhand in Natural Calamity occurred on 7th February 2021"- On request of the State
Government of Uttarakhand to formulate a Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for issuing death
certificate after due inquiry in case of the missing persons of Uttarakhand and other States due to
natural calamity occurred on 07th February,2021; specific procedure prepared and communicated
to the Chief Registrars of all States/UTs for necessary action vide this office circular number 1/2/
(Uttarakhand)/2011-VS-CRS dated 21-02-2021. It was communicated that in case of persons,
whose dead body was found, the normal process of registration of death and issuing death
certificates as prescribed under the RBD Act, 1969 should be followed. Whereas, in case of
missing persons, who in all likelihood have died but the dead body is not traceable, all reasonable
efforts should be made to determine that the person has in all likelihood died in the natural calamity
that occurred in Uttarakhand during Feb., 2021, the death certificate to be issued after following
the due prescribed process and inquiry.

 "Advisory regarding registration of births and deaths"- A few State Governments had
reported issuance of fake birth and death certificates including misuse of User/Login ID and
password created in the existing portal/software developed for online registration of birth and
death, by unauthorized persons. In this regard, an advisory was issued to all States/UTs vide this
office letter no. 1/12/2018 VS (CRS) dated 28-07-2021 for taking precautionary measures in
registration of birth and death in order to avoid misuse of online portal credentials i.e. User/Login

                                                                          29
IDs and passwords. In the said advisory, the State Governments and Administrators were requested
to issue necessary instructions to all the registration authorities, not to share password with any
person, periodically change the password and update the credentials in existing portal/software
developed for online registration of births and deaths.
 In spite of aforesaid advisory, few instances were reported by States/UTs that existing
portal/software developed for online registration of birth and death has come under phishing
attacks. Thus, fake portal/websites were designed with the aim to steal and misuse User/Login ID
and password for issuance of fake birth and death certificates. Consequently, some criminal cases
were registered by the concerned registration authorities. In this respect, all States/UTs were
requested to take up such matter with the concerned authority to conduct a proper inquiry and to
take appropriate action required under the law. The general instructions for handling of login ID
and password along with Dos and Don'ts for the users of Portal/website were circulated to all
States/UTs vide this office letter no. 1/12/2018 VS (CRS) on the subject "Advisory regarding
registration of births and deaths" dated 24-12-2021.
 The aforesaid circulars/letters along with other circulars issued by this office from time to time
may be seen at this office website www.crsorgi.gov.in.

                                                         

                                                                          30
                                                   CHAPTER ­ III

                              A BRIEF ANALYSIS OF DATA ON REGISTERED
                                  BIRTHS, DEATHS AND INFANT DEATHS

3.1 Births and deaths are the two most important vital events in the life of an individual
and for the society as a whole. Based on the data collected under the Civil Registration
System on registered births and deaths, this chapter presents the trend in number of
registered events and other related indicators, at the State and National level.

3.2 In 2021, the number of registration units along with the level of reporting at the
national level and the States/UTs with rural/urban details may be seen at Statement 5. It
may be noted here that level of reporting indicates the status of returns of registered births
and deaths by the concerned Registrar within a State / UT during the year. It provides the
deficiency at a glance and helps in reviewing the registration system and taking measures
that would be necessary to improve the registration levels, wherever required.

3.3 At the national level, 97.6% of the registration units reported monthly returns of
registered births and deaths to the District Registrar's or Chief Registrar's Office during
2021. For rural areas, the reporting level are at 97.6% and for urban areas, it is 99.4%.
However, these figures do not include Arunachal Pradesh and Delhi as these States/UTs
have provided only partial information.

3.4 Cent percent level of reporting have been achieved by 24 States/UTs viz. Andhra

Bengal, A & N Islands, Chandigarh, D & N Haveli and Daman & Diu, Ladakh, Delhi,
Lakshadweep and Puducherry.

3.6 The above data provides details of the number of registered births and deaths over the
years. The number of registered births has decreased slightly from 242.2 lakhs in 2020 to
242.0 lakhs in 2021 ­ a decrease of about 0.1%. In the case of registered births, there has been
a fall in the year 2021 in almost all States/UTs except Andhra Pradesh, Arunachal Pradesh,
Bihar, Himachal Pradesh, Maharashtra, Mizoram, Nagaland, Punjab, Rajasthan, Tripura,
Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, West Bengal, Jammu & Kashmir, Ladakh, and Puducherry
compared to 2020. Twelve States/UTs namely Jharkhand, Karnataka, Assam, Gujarat, Madhya
Pradesh, Telangana, Haryana, Chhattisgarh, Delhi, Kerala, Odisha, and Tamil Nadu have
contributed significantly to the decrease in Registered Births in 2021 over 2020. On the other

                         33
hand, increase in Registered Births in three major States namely, Uttar Pradesh, Bihar and
West Bengal is in six figures during 2020-2021.

3.7 On the other hand, in the case of registered deaths, the number of registered events has
gone up from 81.2 lakhs in 2020 to 102.2 lakhs in 2021, i.e. an increase of 26.0%. Of the 32

Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu, Madhya Pradesh, Karnataka, Bihar, Andhra Pradesh, Kerala, West
Bengal, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Haryana, Punjab, Telangana, Jharkhand and Delhi have
contributed significantly in the increased number of Deaths Registered from 2020 to 2021.

3.9 Registration of Birth & Death within the prescribed time limit of 21 days

 3.9.1 The registered births and deaths have been classified in four categories depending upon
 their period of registration. The four time periods considered for the purpose are: i) within the
 prescribed time limit (up to 21 days), ii) After 21 days but within 30 days, iii) After 30 days but
 within 1 year, iv) More than 1 year. For 2021, Thirty-five (35) States/UTs have provided data
 on Births and Deaths Registration within the prescribed time limit (i.e. within 21 days). In
 respect of both Time Gap Birth and Death, partial information has been submitted by Sikkim
 and therefore has not been taken into account while consolidating the data for Time gap in the
 Registration of Births & Deaths tables.

out of the total registration undertaken in 2021 within the prescribed time limit (21 days) is as

3.12 Medical attention received at the time of birth and death

3.12.1 The complete information in respect of birth by type of medical attention at the time of
delivery and Medical Attention received by deceased at the time of death for 2021 has been
received from only 34 States/UTs. As partial information has been submitted by two States
namely, Maharashtra and Sikkim, their data has not been taken into account while
consolidating the data. In the case of Punjab, the relevant information is excluding cases
registered more than 1 year. The distribution of medical attention received at the time of birth
for these States/UTs has been illustrated in Chart 9. While the data shows that majority of
births have occurred in an institution, either in Government or private hospitals. Institutional
births occurred in about 72.9% of cases, 8.5% of the cases were attended by the
Physicians/Nurse/Midwife. However, about 9.7% of the events are still attended by untrained
Midwife/Dai and another 8.9% by others and Not stated/Not Available taken together.

3.12.2 On the other hand, in the case of Medical Attention received by deceased at the time of
death, it is evident from Chart 10 that 26.5% of the total registered deaths have occurred in
institutions and is on a higher side than that of other places from where the deceased had
received medical attention. About 16.9% of Registered Deaths were reported under Medical
Attention Other than Institutions. About 1.4% of the total registered deaths during 2021 have
received medical attention from qualified allopathic professional and practitioner of other

                                                                           44
system taken together and 47.3% of deceased did not receive any medical attention at the time
of death.

3.14 Still Births

3.14.1 The World Health Organization (WHO) defines a still birth as a baby born with no signs
of life at or after 28 weeks' gestation. Still birth, also known as foetal death, has been defined as
`death prior to the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of human
conception, irrespective of the duration of pregnancy and which is not an induced termination of
pregnancy. The death is indicated by the fact that after such expulsion or extraction, the fetus
does not breathe or show any other evidence of life, such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the
umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles. Heartbeats are to be distinguished
from transient cardiac contractions; stillbirths are to be distinguished from fleeting respiratory
efforts or gasps.'

3.14.2 Statement 17 presents still births registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of
the event for the year 2021 and it may be observed that in total registered still births, share of
rural registered still births is only 35.0% which may be due to poor netting of stillbirths in
rural areas. In urban areas, this share is about 65.0% which may be due to better netting of still
births from medical institutions. State/UT-wise registered still births have been depicted in
Table 4.

             Statement 17: Still Births registered during 2021

Place of Occurrence  No. of Registered Still Births   % Share of Registered Still
                                                     Births by Place of Occurrence

      Rural                    43305                       35.0

      Urban                    80462                       65.0
      Total                    123767                      100.0

3.16 Time Gap in Registration of Birth & Death

3.16.1 On the basis of data made available by States/UTs, the national data table on time gap of
birth and death registrations i.e. within time limit, more than 21 days but within 30 days, more
than 30 days but within 1 year and after one year has been prepared and may be seen in
Statement 19 and 20. Sikkim provided only partial data on Time Gap Births and Deaths
Registration and therefore not included.
